## Work log (append-only)
- 2026-08-07 (FIRST REAL ORDER STRANDED — ROOT CAUSE FOUND, ORDER HEALED, HOLE CLOSED): **Root cause: Stripe was POSTing to a URL deleted 11 days earlier.** Endpoint `we_1SkSOEIK5lihoSZtO5fqfbU3` pointed at `https://api.imaginethisprinted.com/api/webhooks/stripe`, which returns **HTTP 404 "Route not found"** — that route was removed 2026-07-27 in **f4785ce** ("Payments hardening: server-authoritative checkout + one Stripe webhook"), consolidated into `POST /api/stripe/webhook`, and the Stripe Dashboard URL was never updated. Proof, not inference: `evt_3U1wrCIK5lihoSZt0VXcgvkn` (payment_intent.succeeded) carried **`pending_webhooks=2`** — Stripe had never received a 2xx from either registered endpoint — and live probes returned 404 for the configured path vs 400 (signature, i.e. alive) for the real one. A SECOND endpoint, `https://davidtrinidad.com/api/stripe/webhook` (`we_1T02Zw...`, created 2026-02-12, wrong project — the dashboard site) also 404s and has been silently swallowing ITP's live payment events; **left in place, flagged for David** since Ethan Dunn's unmerged commission-ledger work may intend that path. Money was never at risk: `pi_3U1wrCIK5lihoSZt09JA8sL4` was `succeeded`, livemode, charge `ch_3U1wrCIK5lihoSZt0o0Tu5zK`. But because `handleCheckoutOrderPayment` never ran, nothing downstream of "paid" ran: order stuck `status=pending`/`payment_status=pending`/`charge_id=null`, **zero emails ever sent to the customer** (verified against Resend, not just our DB — `utils/email.ts sendEmail` does NOT write `email_messages`, so DB absence proves nothing), no rewards, no blank-inventory decrement, no creator margins, no merch-ledger emission. The daily ops digest at 12:19 that day reported **"0 orders / $0.00 last 24h"** because it counts `payment_status='paid'`. Broken for EVERY real payment since 2026-07-27, storefront/Merch Studio Checkout Sessions included (they bridge to the same `payment_intent.succeeded` via `payment_intent_data.metadata`, routes/storefront.ts:397).
  **LIVE FIXES APPLIED (Stripe-side, in effect now, no deploy needed):** repointed `we_1SkSOE...` to `/api/stripe/webhook` and widened `enabled_events` 9 -> 19 as a UNION (never a replacement, so nothing already subscribed was dropped) to cover every event the code handles — `payment_intent.canceled`, `charge.refunded`, all five `charge.dispute.*` and `invoice.*` were all missing. Then replayed the stranded event with a correctly-computed `t=...,v1=HMAC-SHA256(secret, "ts.payload")` signature -> **HTTP 200 `{"received":true}`**, which also PROVED Render's `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` matches this endpoint (otherwise the URL fix would have traded a 404 for a 400). Verified after: order now `status=processing`/`payment_status=paid`, and Resend shows the customer's confirmation **delivered** at 23:54:47 ("Your Ember Warrior tee is en route!"). `order_rewards` is legitimately 0 — she checked out as a guest (`user_id` null).
  **CODE FIXES (branch only, NOT deployed):** (1) The paid-order pipeline moved verbatim out of `routes/stripe.ts` into NEW `services/order-payment.ts` as `applyPaidCheckoutOrder(pi, log, source)` — it had exactly ONE caller, which is precisely why a dead webhook left the system with no other way to finish a paid order; `req` became `log` because that is the only thing it ever used `req` for; the atomic `UPDATE ... WHERE payment_status != 'paid'` claim and every fail-soft catch are unchanged deliberately. (2) NEW `reconcileUnrecordedPayments()` on the worker's hourly loop asks Stripe directly — "is this PaymentIntent succeeded?" — for orders we still believe unpaid (14-day window, 25/run, both env-configurable; excludes cancelled/refunded), heals them through the identical function, and raises a `health_alert` naming the recovered orders and pointing at the endpoint config. Runs FIRST in the loop so a healed order counts as paid in the same run's stall check and digest. (3) NEW `sendNewOrderTeamEmail` + a `new_order` `admin_notifications` row fire the moment an order is paid — **this did not exist at all**; the only team-facing order signals were the 3-DAY stall alert and the 8am digest, which is exactly why nobody was told. Reconciler-sourced orders carry an explicit "Stripe's webhook did NOT deliver" banner so a broken pipeline cannot read as a normal sale. (4) `charge_id` is now captured on the claim — it was NULL forever, leaving `findOrderForCharge`'s charge_id fallback permanently dead. (5) Fixed an inherited bug in the moved code: confirmation emails itemised every line at **$0.00** because they read `item.price`, a column that does not exist (`order_items` has `unit_price`). (6) `src/pages/OrderSuccess.tsx` never fetched the order — it sliced the raw UUID to fake an order number (customer saw "BF1ABB5F", not `ITP-MSJK1K3I-8GDG`) and rendered a **hardcoded** green "Processing" pill regardless of reality, which is literally the "it still says processing" David saw. It now polls the already-existing, guest-safe `GET /api/orders/:orderId/confirmation` (built for exactly this and never given a caller) and derives the pill from real `payment_status`/`status`, degrading to "Confirming" rather than an error page.
  **SHIPPING CHOICE (request 2):** `POST /checkout-payment-intent` destructured only `shippingType`/`rush`/`shippingQuoteToken`, used them to PRICE the order and dropped the rest. `shippingMethod`, `pickupAppointment`, `rushFee`, `isLocalDelivery` were never even destructured, and nothing was persisted — so Order Management could show a dollar amount but not what the customer chose, and a $0 line was ambiguous between pickup and free-shipping-over-$50. NEW exported `snapshotShippingChoice()` writes `orders.metadata.shipping` (method/type/rush/rush_fee/amount/free_shipping_applied/is_local_delivery/pickup_appointment) on BOTH the create and update paths, with a hard trust boundary: every number comes from the server's pricing result (`rushFeeCents` newly surfaced on `OrderPricingResult`), `type` is whitelist-validated, and the client's `method` is display-only, trimmed and capped at 120 chars; appointments attach ONLY to pickups. Also mirrored onto the PaymentIntent metadata (`shippingMethod`, `shippingKind`) so the choice survives in Stripe. **Destiny's order specifically: provably Local Pickup, no rush, by elimination** — subtotal $25 is under the $50 free-shipping threshold, local delivery must be exactly $10 or $15, a failed quote verification would have 400'd instead of creating an order, and she used no coupon, so `shipping_amount = 0` is reachable ONLY through the pickup branch (`resolveShipping`, order-pricing.ts:432). Her chosen pickup **date/time/notes are unrecoverable** — never stored anywhere, including Stripe. Deliberately NOT backfilled: writing an inferred value into `metadata.shipping` would present a deduction as recorded fact, so the UI shows "not recorded" instead.
  **"MANAGE ORDER SHOWS ME NOTHING" (request 3):** the modal rendered Order Details (4 numbers), the address, Status Management and notes — and for line items, a **COUNT**. No design, no size, no colour, no print location, no artwork link; the crew could not work an order from it. Worse, the item mapping read FOUR columns that do not exist: `item.price`, `item.total`, `item.variations`, `item.personalization` (real schema is `unit_price`/`subtotal`/`metadata` jsonb), plus `images: []` hardcoded — so unit price and design URL were undefined on every line regardless. Added a **Production — what to make** panel above Status Management: per line, the mockup thumbnail, name, `qty x unit = subtotal`, and spec chips for QTY / Size / Colour (with a swatch when the value is a hex) / Print location, where a MISSING size or print location renders amber "NOT SET" rather than blank; add-ons listed; and press-ready-artwork buttons — one per placement from `metadata.print_files`, else a single design download, else an explicit amber "No print-ready file on this line — use the product artwork" (which is the correct, honest state for a catalogue product like Destiny's tee). Also surfaces `orders.metadata.print` (the Watchtower print-factory status) when present. `backend/routes/orders.ts` GET / had a metadata->order_items fallback that emitted the OLD broken shape, which would have defeated the whole panel whenever `order_items` was empty; it now emits the SAME shape the real table has, tagged `metadata.from_snapshot: true`. The equivalent fallbacks in `/my` and `/:orderId` were deliberately NOT touched — they normalise to `variations`/`personalization` for the customer-facing MyOrders page and have their own consumers.
  **DID THE TEAM GET AN EMAIL (request 4): NO.** Production was running the pre-fix code when the event was replayed, so only the customer was mailed. Verified against Resend: the sole message in that window went to tiny4295@gmail.com. Also found: `PRINT_WORKER_EMAILS=wecare@imaginethisprinted.com` — the shared company mailbox only, no individual crew addresses, so even once deployed the alert lands in one inbox until that list is widened.
  VERIFY: backend `tsc --noEmit` EXIT 0; frontend `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` EXIT 0; `npm run build` PASSED (10.00s); **backend suite 358 tests / 33 files ALL PASS**, up from the 339/31 baseline — 19 NEW tests: 8 pinning the reconciler (heals only on `succeeded`, leaves an abandoned `requires_payment_method` checkout completely alone, alarms loudly on heal, survives one bad intent lookup, does not count a lost idempotency race, no-ops when nothing is outstanding, no throw on query failure, skips without a Stripe key) and 11 pinning the shipping trust boundary (pickup-vs-free-shipping ambiguity, rush from the server fee not the client claim, cents->dollars, label fallbacks, unknown type rejected, hostile 500-char label capped, appointment never attached to a non-pickup). eslint measured against HEAD via `git show | eslint --stdin` (no stash — other sessions have work in this tree): stripe.ts **36 -> 32** (code moved out), order-monitor.ts 5 -> 7, email.ts 2 -> 3, OrderSuccess.tsx 0 -> 0; new files 10 and 13 — all `no-explicit-any`, matching each file's existing style, 0 new rule classes.
  **NOT DEPLOYED — and the split matters:** the Stripe endpoint repoint is LIVE, so the next real order WILL be marked paid and WILL get its customer confirmation with no deploy. The team alert, the reconciler, the shipping capture and all three UI fixes are code on this branch only and do nothing until merge + deploy. Also NOT done: no migration was added (the shipping snapshot deliberately rides `metadata` jsonb to avoid a prod DDL step); the stray `davidtrinidad.com` endpoint is still registered and still 404ing; the reconciler has never run against live data; and nothing was committed — the working tree carries these changes alongside other sessions' edits.
- 2026-08-07 (TEAM ALERT FIRED FOR THE LIVE ORDER + a CHECK-constraint bug caught by doing it): David chose "send now", so the NEW `sendNewOrderTeamEmail` was run against the already-paid order from a temp `tsx` script using the real production template — **email SENT and accepted by Resend (id 6f2198f9-e8a7-41c6-ba76-bb94fdccda3e) to wecare@imaginethisprinted.com**, carrying the $26 total, the 1x Ember Warrior Graphic Tee XL / black / front_image line, the Powder Springs address, and a green LOCAL PICKUP fulfilment block. The pickup method was passed in explicitly and **labelled "deduced from the $0 shipping charge, NOT recorded at checkout"** — `orders.metadata.shipping` was deliberately left absent rather than backfilled, so no inference is stored as fact. **The bell row FAILED, and that is a bug this exercise caught in code that would otherwise have shipped:** `insert({type:'new_order'})` violates `admin_notifications_type_check`, whose whitelist is a fixed list (`new_ticket, ticket_reply, ticket_escalation, agent_needed, low_stock, order_stalled, health_alert, wholesale_application`) and has never included `new_order`. Would have failed identically in production — silently, since `notifyTeamOfPaidOrder` wraps the insert in its own try/catch. Fixed by NEW `supabase/migrations/20260807_admin_notifications_new_order.sql`, using the same DROP/ADD restatement pattern the constraint has already been widened with three times (20260706 blank_inventory, 20260727 refunds_and_disputes, 20260728 wholesale_applications); verified against the two untracked 20260805/20260806 security migrations — they only touch admin_notifications RLS policies, no constraint conflict. The reconciler's own alert uses `health_alert`, which was already legal, so that path was never affected. **MUST BE APPLIED TO PROD** — until then every paid order sends the crew email but logs a failed bell insert. Decisions recorded: David will contact Destiny about her pickup time himself (no customer email drafted or sent from this session); `PRINT_WORKER_EMAILS` remains the single shared `wecare@` mailbox, so widening it to individual crew inboxes is still open. Nothing was committed — all changes sit in the working tree alongside other sessions' edits.
- 2026-08-07 (ADMIN NOTIFICATION BELL — dropdown was trapped inside the header banner): David reported "the noti bell and notis are on the inside" with a screenshot showing the panel sliced off at the purple banner's bottom edge, only the "Notifications / Mark all as read" row and one sliver of a ticket visible. **Scope expansion — direct owner request on the admin UI** (the standing CLAUDE_TASK brief is the finished Etsy-email task and is stale; same owner-request pattern as the 2026-06-29 and 2026-07-06 expansions). Root cause was NOT z-index: `src/pages/AdminDashboard.tsx:1816` carried `overflow-hidden` on the gradient header wrapper, and an `overflow: hidden` ancestor clips absolutely-positioned descendants no matter what their z-index is — so the `z-50` on both the bell wrapper and the panel were dead weight. Moved `overflow-hidden` down onto the decorative SVG-pattern layer (`absolute inset-0 opacity-10`), which is what it was ever meant to clip, leaving the header itself unclipped. Verified the stacking still works after freeing it: the header's inner `relative z-10` is a positioned stacking context while the main content div below is static, so the panel paints above the stat cards and tab bar (confirmed visually, not just reasoned). **Second, adjacent defect fixed:** the bell button was `text-muted` on a saturated purple gradient and was effectively invisible — in the before-screenshot only the red "61" badge read at all. Rather than hardcode white into a shared component, added an optional `buttonClassName` prop to `src/components/AdminNotificationBell.tsx` defaulting to the existing `text-muted hover:text-text` (so any future light-background mount is unchanged), and AdminDashboard now passes `text-white/90 hover:text-white bg-white/10 hover:bg-white/20` plus the button gained `rounded-lg` for the chip. VERIFY: reproduced the clip live at localhost:5173/admin before the fix (panel cut at y=136, exactly the banner edge), re-verified after — full list renders over the cards, internal `max-h-96` scroll intact, "View all tickets" footer reachable, bell legible on the gradient; `npx tsc -b --noEmit` EXIT 0. Pre-existing and NOT touched: the admin page logs "Error loading 3D models" and "Error loading vendor products" on mount, unrelated to this change (likely anon-access fallout from the untracked 20260805/20260806 security-lockdown migrations). Nothing committed — changes sit in the working tree alongside other sessions' edits.
- 2026-08-07 (SUPPORT QUEUE HARD-PURGED from live prod, on explicit owner instruction, with a snapshot taken first): David asked to "clear everything in the support tickets". The phrase was genuinely ambiguous across three materially different and differently-reversible actions, so nothing was touched until he picked one. Read-only inventory first (service role, live prod): `support_tickets` 66 rows = **4 open / 62 closed**; `admin_notifications` 66 rows = **61 unread** (the "61" bell badge); `ticket_messages` 1 row. The important finding was that **the bell's noise and the ticket queue's contents were not the same thing** — the 61 unread notifications were the gibberish-subject spam wave (e.g. `AgxGKnFfiJwgYAGeR` from tomharaske@aol.com), but those tickets had ALREADY been closed, so the open queue was only 4 rows and all 4 were real: `Sign-in issue - buffering` (info@davidtrinidad.com, Dec 10), `Trouble logging into email` (Dec 18), `Trouble logging in` (Dec 19 — the single `ticket_messages` row is this customer replying "okay waiting", never answered), and `test` from Gretchen Myers (Apr 30, body just "hi christina"). Also confirmed **no delete/bulk-close endpoint exists anywhere in the codebase** (`backend/routes/admin/support.ts` exposes only get/reply/status/claim/release/escalate/notifications), so any purge had to be a direct service-role write with no application path and no undo. Presented four options with those tradeoffs stated — including that a blanket wipe buries three unanswered December login complaints — and **David chose the full hard delete**; that is his call and it was executed in full. Ran a snapshot-then-purge script that exports all three tables to JSON, re-reads the file and compares row counts, and ABORTS before deleting if the round-trip does not match; deletes then ran child-first (`ticket_messages` -> `admin_notifications` -> `support_tickets`) so FKs could never block. VERIFY: backup wrote and round-tripped at 66/1/66; post-delete counts re-queried = **0 / 0 / 0**; UI re-checked live — bell badge gone with the "No notifications yet" empty state rendering (and still correctly escaping the header, confirming today's overflow fix on an empty list too), Support tab renders zero rows with no crash or stuck spinner. **BACKUP IS THE ONLY COPY OF THAT DATA and it is NOT in the repo** — `E:/Projects for MetaSphere/itp-support-backup-2026-08-07.json` (63K), copied off the session scratchpad deliberately because scratchpad lives under AppData\Local\Temp and gets reaped. Note this also deleted non-ticket notifications that shared the table (the `order_stalled` rows for the two 196-day-stalled orders), which was covered by the chosen option's wording. Nothing committed.
- 2026-08-08 (STRAY WEBHOOK ENDPOINT DISABLED — David: "only the stripe connect should be there not the other one"): Inspected both endpoints on ITP's live account before touching either, which settled the question factually. `we_1T02ZwIK5lihoSZtzAss3zbm` -> `https://davidtrinidad.com/api/stripe/webhook` (created 2026-02-12, `application: null`, i.e. a plain account endpoint and NOT a Connect-app endpoint) carried **ZERO Connect events** — its six events were `payment_intent.succeeded`, `payment_intent.payment_failed`, `invoice.paid`, `invoice.payment_failed`, `charge.succeeded`, `charge.refunded`, all ITP storefront payment traffic, all landing on a 404. Meanwhile the surviving ITP endpoint `we_1SkSOEIK5lihoSZtO5fqfbU3` **already carries all five Connect events** (`account.updated`, `payout.created/updated/paid/failed`), so ITP's Connect work — vendor payouts and ITC cashouts via `services/stripe-connect.ts` — was never dependent on the davidtrinidad.com endpoint at all. Set the stray endpoint to **`status: disabled`** rather than deleting it: reversible with a single `disabled=false`, it preserves the config and signing secret for Ethan to inspect, and it immediately stops ITP's live payment events being delivered into the wrong project. Stamped its Stripe `description` with the reason and a pointer to the surviving endpoint id so anyone reading the Dashboard sees why. Verified after the change: stray = `disabled`; survivor = `enabled`, 19 events, all 5 Connect events intact, `payment_intent.succeeded` present. David is having Ethan choose the correct URL for whatever the dashboard/commission-ledger side actually needs. NOTE for whoever picks that up: `payout.created` and `payout.updated` are subscribed on ITP's endpoint but NOT handled in `routes/stripe.ts` — they fall to the log-and-ignore default branch. Harmless (they pre-date this work and were preserved by the union) but they are noise in the logs.

SCREENSHOTS: `/api/watchtower/tasks` accepts multipart with an optional `screenshot` image (multer memory, 8MB, image-only filter; JSON bodies from Mr. Imagine's tool skip multer untouched); bytes are magic-byte sniffed (sniffImageContentType — never trust client type), uploaded to GCS `watchtower-tasks/<uuid>.<ext>`, and stapled to the description as trailing `Screenshot: <url>` (the board already renders those on task cards); upload failure = 502 + task NOT filed, never a silent screenshotless task. Widget: attach button + Ctrl+V paste-to-attach (Win+Shift+S flow), preview with remove, 8MB guard, and submit switched from apiFetch (forces JSON content-type) to hand-built FormData fetch with the Supabase bearer. - 2026-07-31 (migration apply script): Added `scripts/apply-pending-migrations.mjs` — applies ONLY the four files `MIGRATION_LEDGER.md` proved missing from prod, in the one safe order. Lives at repo root, not `backend/scripts/`, because `pg` is declared in the ROOT package.json only — `backend/scripts/run-migration-pg.ts` imports it and works today purely by node_modules hoisting, which a backend-only `npm ci` would break. **DRY RUN BY DEFAULT**: without `--apply` it connects, prints LIVE/PENDING per migration from a read-only probe, prints the ordered plan, and writes nothing. Each migration applies in its OWN transaction and is re-verified INSIDE that transaction before COMMIT, so a file that runs without error but doesn't produce the object it claims rolls back instead of shipping; the first failure rolls back and stops the run. The ledger's ordering rule is ENFORCED IN CODE, not documented: `prevent-role-escalation` and `orders-staff-write` both declare `requires: ['fix-get-user-role']`, and a prerequisite that is neither already live nor applied earlier in the same run aborts — this is the constraint that matters, because that trigger's body calls `get_user_role()`, which raises `42702 ambiguous_column` on every call live today, so applying it alone would break every role-changing UPDATE including David's own admin tooling. Status probes are real catalog reads, not guesses: `get_user_role` is CALLED and the 42702/42883 error codes distinguish "buggy" from "missing"; the trigger is looked up in `pg_trigger` (excluding `tgisinternal`); the orders policies in `pg_policies` by name; `landing_page_suggestions` via `to_regclass`. All four target files are idempotent (CREATE OR REPLACE / DROP..IF EXISTS+CREATE / CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS), so a re-run is safe. Optional `--track` inserts the filename-derived version into `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, and only if that table exists) so future `db push` runs stop retrying; `--only=<id>` restricts the run but does NOT bypass the prerequisite guard. Closes by printing the ledger items it deliberately does NOT touch (`decrement_itc` missing → wallet.ts 500s today; `fix_sync_product_images.sql` never ran) so they don't get forgotten. VERIFY: `node --check` clean; eslint 0 problems; three guard rails exercised for real — missing DATABASE_URL exits with the exact run command, `--only=bogus` lists the valid ids, and a reachable-but-refused DSN proves all four migration files resolve and read non-empty BEFORE any connection is opened. NOT verified against a live database — direct prod DB access was blocked in this session, so the SQL-execution, in-transaction verification and `--track` paths are unexercised; the first real run should be the dry run.

- 2026-08-09 (ORDER MANAGEMENT PRODUCTION FILES + MOCKUP QA + PLACEMENT — three requests, commits e2e3c6f / 7a49075 / bcc099a, all local, NOT pushed): **(1) Downloads.** Order lines showed a thumbnail and at best one print file; mockups/clean PNG/DTF/halftone live on the PRODUCT (`product_assets`), not the order line. New `backend/services/product-files.ts` resolves the bundle for a whole page in ONE batched query. **The trap it exists to hide: halftone is NOT `kind='halftone'`** — `services/image-flow/api/halftone.ts` writes `kind='source'` with `asset_role='design_halftone'`, so a kind-only query returns the CLEAN design labelled as the press file; both are valid PNGs on the same product so nothing downstream would catch a shirt printed from the wrong one. Role is the discriminator and halftone is matched BEFORE the design lookup. Legacy `products.metadata.assets` + `metadata.mockup_url` fold in behind one contract, gap-fill only. Live-data audit before building: of 1000 products only 3 carry `metadata.assets`, **0 halftones exist anywhere** (endpoint works, never run), and the ONLY product ever ordered has zero assets — so a passive halftone button would have been permanently empty; David chose a Generate button (local engine, deterministic, $0), gated to admins because `/api/image-flow/halftone` is `requireAdmin` while the page also serves manager/founder. 14 tests, one of which caught a real pre-ship defect: `attachProductFiles` early-returned raw orders when no line resolved to a product, leaving `product_files` undefined and the UI reading `.mockups` off it — exactly the shape of the only live order. **(2) Pocket mockup.** The generator already supported pocket scale (`printPlacement: 'left-pocket'` → "small, positioned on the left chest pocket area"), so this needed a job not a template — but `asset_role` was derived from TEMPLATE ALONE and the writer deletes any existing asset for that role, so a pocket `flat_lay` would have deleted the front `flat_lay` and the next front run the pocket: one survivor forever, silently, no error. Placement is now part of a mockup's identity (`mockup_pocket`, order 4, barred from primary). **(3) Mockup QA.** `verifyDesignFidelity` already existed but ONLY in the Etsy shoot; generalised into `backend/services/mockup-qa.ts` and now runs on every builder mockup, plus a NEW independent coverage gate (David: "should not cover the entire shirt unless specified") — judged against the placement the product was BUILT for, so back-only stays large and all-over is exempt rather than being rejected for being correct. ONE corrective retry with the reason fed back via new `RunMockupOpts.retryNote` (a blind re-roll mostly reproduces the same failure); a second failure lands FLAGGED on the asset (`qa_ok`/`qa_failed`/`qa_reason`), never discarded; QA unavailable = PASS so an outage can't bin a paid render. **(4) Etsy shots joined the set** — they lived only on `metadata.etsy_shots` so NOTHING that reads mockups could see them; now mirrored to `product_assets` as `mockup_model_1/2` on first shoot and reshoot, QA-failed shots deliberately not mirrored (skips logged). Model shots are now kicked off from the builder fan-out for garments. **(5) The silent-invisibility catch:** `buildProductGallery`'s ROLE_ORDER is a WHITELIST not a sort, so the pocket shot and both model shots would have been generated, stored and NEVER displayed anywhere; added (pocket after the front shots so it's never the hero) and pinned with 8 tests. The customer placement selector already existed on ProductPage (`front_image|back_image|pocket`, buttons not a dropdown, shown only when >1 location) — the real gap was that picking a placement didn't change the image, so Pocket now jumps the gallery to the pocket render. - 2026-08-11 (MINIMAX VOICE + GROK COST CONTAINMENT — David: "someone uses all my grok api talking to mr imagine" + "i like his minimax voice better then grok… we keep the studio but make it clean mr imagine face on side he response minimax dictation"): a full review first (2 Explore agents + live prod DB probes), then the build. **THE REVIEW'S CORE FINDING — the two asks had ONE answer.** Grok realtime bills per wall-clock MINUTE the socket is open, and the browser connects to xAI DIRECTLY, so after the server mints an ephemeral token it cannot cap session length, concurrency, or minutes; the 6/min limiter capped how often you START, not how long you RUN. `grok-voice-latest` re-aliased to Think Fast 2.0 on 2026-08-05 → **$0.08/min ($4.80/hr)**, so one idle tab overnight ≈ $38. Worse: `session.update` sends voice+instructions+tools FROM THE BROWSER (useMrImagineLive.ts:246-259), so a token holder can discard the persona and use raw Grok realtime; the token is bearer-only, bound to no user/IP/origin, so it can be shared. Pool = anyone with an email (instant creator opt-in). And NEITHER token route logged a successful mint, so a spend spike was unattributable. Containment that did exist: ITP has a dedicated xAI key (vault itp.XAI_API_KEY), ACL wildcard `model:*`/`endpoint:*`. **FIX — the customer lane no longer mints Grok tokens AT ALL; the route is DELETED, not mitigated.** Admin studio keeps realtime (routes/ai/realtime.ts) where the pool is a few trusted staff, now with a mint log line. **NEW `POST /api/creator/studio/turn`** is the customer lane: multipart audio → GCS → `transcribeAudio` (gpt-4o-transcribe) → OpenAI chat WITH TOOLS → **MiniMax `generateConversationalResponse` in Mr. Imagine's own CLONED voice** (`AVAILABLE_VOICES.MR_IMAGINE = moss_audio_737a299c…`, which already existed and is what David prefers). Cost is per UTTERANCE (~$0.20-0.30 per 10-min chat vs $0.80 on realtime) and **idle is free**. **Division of labour, deliberate:** /turn is BRAIN+VOICE only — when Mr. Imagine decides to spend he returns an `action` and the BROWSER calls the existing /create, /select-image, /submit endpoints, which already carry the ITC charge, creator gate and ownership scoping. One money path, not two. Pure-state tools (set_product_type/set_design_brief/get_pricing) come back as a `statePatch`. Voice plays BEFORE the action fires so "this costs 40 ITC, here we go" is heard before the charge. NEW `src/hooks/useMrImagineVoice.ts` (tap-to-talk, MediaRecorder, <1200-byte guard, mute persisted, typing fallback for quiet rooms/denied mics). CreatorStudio.tsx rewritten: **his face on the side reacting to state** — art swaps per status (waist-up / thinking / happy), glow colour shifts green-listening → amber-thinking → purple-speaking, ping ring while recording, bouncing dots + float while speaking, last line in a bubble. **3D lane honesty fix:** the old page could start a concept but could never convert it (it never fetched size tiers and /approve doesn't return them), so the studio now hands off to the Toy Creator, which already does approve+size+convert properly — persona updated to match rather than promising something it can't do. **HARDENING:** select-image gained a REPLAY GUARD (409 if mockups already exist or the product is submitted — it was re-runnable, each call ~4-6 renders + a model shoot) and a MAX_PICKS cap of 4; /api/ai/transcribe gained a 12 MB upload cap + 20/min limiter (it had neither); realtime /token now logs userId on success. VERIFY: backend tsc 0 + **399/399**, frontend tsc 0, production build clean (14.1s). NOT DONE — needs David: **the ITC self-mint hole is still open** (`supabase/migrations/20260810_lock_wallet_balance.sql` written but NOT applied; live `user_wallets` has two UPDATE policies with no WITH CHECK and no guarding trigger, so any user can write their own balance — verified against the live DB, pre-existing since migration 002, and it means ITC gates nothing anywhere). Also NOT live-tested: no real creator has run a MiniMax turn yet ($ spend on transcribe+TTS).

## File shortlist (approved scope) — 2026-07-26 transactional email fix (Watchtower 76112294)
Scope expanded beyond the two files named in the dispatch because the acceptance
criterion "guest buyers can click the CTA and view their order status without
logging in" cannot be met inside `email.ts` alone — a link needs a public
endpoint and a public page to land on. `/orders` (the old CTA) is the ADMIN
OrderManagement route, and `/account/orders` is behind `ProtectedRoute`.

### Read first
- `backend/routes/stripe.ts` (order confirmation + status-update email sends)
- `backend/utils/email.ts` (order confirmation / shipped / delivered templates)
- `backend/services/emailAI.ts` (the AI path these emails prefer when enabled)
- `src/pages/MyOrders.tsx` (existing customer-facing carrier detection to mirror)

### Edit allowed
- `backend/routes/stripe.ts`
- `backend/utils/email.ts`
- `backend/services/emailAI.ts`
- `backend/utils/carrier-tracking.ts` (add — shared carrier deep links)
- `backend/utils/order-status-token.ts` (add — HMAC guest-link tokens)
- `backend/routes/orders.ts` (add public `GET /status/:orderId`)
- `backend/scripts/verify-order-emails.ts` (add — credential-free verification)
- `src/pages/OrderStatus.tsx` (add — public guest status page)
- `src/App.tsx` (register the public route)

- 2026-07-26 (Transactional order emails — friendly order number, real customer name, carrier deep links, guest status link; Watchtower 76112294, amelia-chan): Every customer-facing order email was identifying orders by the primary-key UUID and sending buyers to a page they cannot open. `stripe.ts` passed `order.id` into `sendOrderEmail` with no name, so the subject read `Order Confirmed - 3F1C9D2E`, the body printed the same 8-char slice, the greeting was "Hey there, creative soul!" and the CTA pointed at `/orders` — which is the ADMIN OrderManagement route, not the customer's orders (those live at `/account/orders`, behind ProtectedRoute, so a guest buyer had no reachable destination at all). Shipped/delivered emails had the same defects plus tracking numbers rendered as plain `<strong>` text with no link. FIXED: all three templates (confirmation, shipped, delivered) plus the AI path in `emailAI.ts` now take `orders.order_number` as the customer-facing identifier and `orders.customer_name` for the greeting, with the uuid carried separately in a new `OrderEmailOptions.orderId` purely to mint links; a `displayOrderNumber()` guard still degrades to the old slice if a legacy caller hands over a raw uuid, and all interpolated order/customer values are HTML-escaped. New `backend/utils/carrier-tracking.ts` resolves carrier + deep link from the free-text `tracking_company` FIRST (substring patterns, USPS tested before UPS because "usps" contains "ups" — an exact-alias table got this wrong and returned FedEx for "USPS Priority Mail"), falling back to tracking-number shape (same regexes `MyOrders.tsx` uses, so site and email never disagree), then to a package search so the button is never dead. New `backend/utils/order-status-token.ts` mints a stateless HMAC (`ORDER_STATUS_TOKEN_SECRET`, falling back through JWT_SECRET / SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY so it works with no new env config) — no DB column, no migration, and deliberately no expiry, since buyers open shipping emails months later. New public `GET /api/orders/status/:orderId?t=` (registered before the auth'd `/:orderId`) verifies the token in constant time, returns a minimal read-only projection with a masked email, and 404s identically on bad token and missing order so existence isn't leaked. New `src/pages/OrderStatus.tsx` at public route `/order-status/:orderId` renders status, a four-step progress rail, the carrier deep link and the line items. VERIFIED: `backend/scripts/verify-order-emails.ts` (`npx tsx`, stubs Resend at the fetch boundary so the real transport path runs) — 30/30 checks pass, asserting the friendly number in subject AND body, no uuid anywhere, "Hey Sarah!" not "Creative Friend", a clickable UPS deep link, and the tokenized CTA. Root `npm run typecheck` exit 0; backend `tsc --noEmit` 247 errors BEFORE and AFTER (all pre-existing `req.log` / uninstalled-dep noise, none in touched files); `npx vite build` emits `OrderStatus-*.js`. NOT verified live: the new endpoint was never hit against a real Supabase row — this worktree has no backend/node_modules and no service-role key. NOTE for whoever deploys: `npm run build` fails at HEAD on `src/utils/storage.ts` / `@aws-sdk/client-s3` under `tsc -b`, unrelated and pre-existing.
- 2026-07-26 (Design-library media durability — Watchtower 1cb2f16c-2a00-46a1-85f6-61971777d00e, dispatched to Iahhm): the importer was writing 365-day GCS signed URLs straight into `products.images[0]`, so all 2,369 imported library listings were set to 403 one year after import; it also recorded `.ai/.svg/.psd` originals as local `E:\` paths (unreachable to the app, the storefront, or the printer bridge) and captured no pixel dimensions or DPI at all. Fixed all three. NEW `backend/routes/media.ts` — a permanent `/api/media/<gcs-path>` address that mints a fresh 1-hour V4 signature per request, with an allowlist (`design-library/`, `design-sources/`, `graphics/`, `stock-models/`, `products/`, `mockups/`) so it can never be turned into an open reader for private `users/**` uploads, a 45-min redirect cache deliberately shorter than the signature TTL, a 60s negative cache, and `?download=1` for save-as on vector sources. Mounted in `index.ts` ahead of `/api/seo`. NEW shared `backend/scripts/lib/design-media.mjs` (URL builder, `readImageStats` via sharp, resumable `uploadSources`). `import-designs.mjs` now stores permanent URLs, uploads the originals to `design-sources/<collection>/<id>.<ext>`, and records `metadata.image`; `--no-sources` / `--include-psd` control the originals tier. NEW `backend/scripts/backfill-design-library-media.mjs` repaired the existing catalogue, idempotent per step and safe to interrupt. RESULT (verified against prod): 2,369/2,369 on permanent URLs, ZERO expiring signed URLs left; 2,369/2,369 carry `metadata.image` {width_px,height_px,dpi,dpi_source,print_inches_at_300dpi,print_quality,printable}; 2,073 products now point at 5,843 uploaded originals with ZERO local paths remaining (the other 296 genuinely ship PNG-only). Route proven locally end-to-end: 302→200 image/png, vector `.ai/.eps/.svg/.psd` all download with correct MIME, non-ASCII filenames round-trip, `users/**` 404s, literal AND percent-encoded `../` 400s. Also fixed a latent trap: every `scripts/*.mjs` used bare `import 'dotenv/config'`, which does NOT override OS env — David's vault loader exports a different `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, so the importer silently authenticated with the wrong key ("Invalid API key"); both scripts now use `dotenv.config({override:true})` exactly as `load-env.ts` does. DECISIONS (owner not consulted, dispatch was hands-off): dimensions live in `metadata.image` JSONB, no migration — `products` metadata is already the established extension point for this importer, and `print_inches_at_300dpi` is the honest quality measure because DPI tags are frequently absent. TWO THINGS DAVID SHOULD KNOW: (1) the permanent URLs 404 until this branch merges and Render redeploys the API — risk is low because all 2,369 are `draft` and the 33 active products were untouched, but nothing can be activated before that deploy (task 35d1c0d1). (2) The comment in `services/gcs-storage.ts` claiming public bucket access is org-blocked is STALE — plain `storage.googleapis.com` GETs on this bucket return 200 today (uniform bucket-level access with a public read binding, which is also why `makePublic()` throws). Direct public URLs would skip the API hop entirely, so `MEDIA_PUBLIC_BASE` + `--force-urls` makes that a one-command switch; left OFF by default because the app's service account cannot read `storage.buckets.getIamPolicy` to confirm that binding is intentional or permanent, and if it is ever tightened every stored URL 403s again — the exact bomb this task existed to defuse. Follow-ups filed: 35d1c0d1 (merge+deploy, blocking), 68abcb40 (importer walks only ONE folder level, so 1,392 designs in double-nested collections like `8. Fishing/Fishing (50 Designs)/Fishing (50 Designs)/` have NEVER been imported — a third of the library), 874932e3 (39 designs measured under 1200px short edge, too small to print; block activation on `metadata.image.printable === false`). Backend `tsc --noEmit` exit 0.

- 2026-07-26 (Ethan Dunn, Watchtower task d8ec0677-5df0-476d-b9cf-f1db4bf6f2e8) — Resend bounce/complaint handling + suppression list. The Resend webhook previously discarded every event that was not `email.received`, so nothing tracked delivery, opens, hard bounces or spam complaints, and the platform kept mailing dead addresses. Added `email_suppressions` (+ atomic `record_email_suppression()` upsert) in `supabase/migrations/20260726_email_suppressions.sql` — APPLIED to the live Supabase DB and smoke-tested (normalization, event_count increment, complaint-outranks-bounce precedence, test rows removed). New `backend/services/email-suppression.ts` owns lookup/record/list; `backend/routes/email.ts` now routes `email.delivered/.opened/.clicked/.bounced/.complained/.failed` onto `email_logs` (joined on `message_id` = the Resend email id, which is what `sendEmailWithTracking` already stores) and flips the matching outbound `email_messages` row to `failed` on a terminal failure. Only PERMANENT bounces and complaints suppress; transient/unclassified bounces are logged, never suppressed. Account-wide Resend webhooks are filtered by sender domain so another product's bounces cannot suppress our customers. `sendEmailWithTracking` now short-circuits suppressed recipients (fails OPEN on lookup error so a DB blip cannot mute order email). Admin UI: read-only "Suppressions" modal on `/admin/email` backed by `GET /api/email/suppressions` (admin/manager). Removal is intentionally not exposed in the UI.
- 2026-08-16 (Rico Fernandez, Watchtower task 435c591b-c069-45dd-aadb-a1083bace1a5) — merged the above (commit e1ea309) into `main` and deployed to Render. See handoff for live-verification detail (bounce test to bounce@simulator.amazonses.com, `email_suppressions` row, admin Suppressions modal).

### Scope expansion — FLUX.2 [pro] migration + single-call mockup prototype (added 2026-07-26 by Dr. Dill, Watchtower task e322a3f2-2ea1-4984-8721-c9ef43093288)
- Rationale: dispatched migration off `black-forest-labs/flux-1.1-pro-ultra` ($0.06/image) to `flux-2-pro` ($0.015/MP in + $0.015/MP out) for cost, plus a prototype collapsing the flat_lay/ghost_mannequin 2-call chain into one multi-reference call. Files below are outside the Etsy shortlist above and are claimed here per the CLAUDE.md scope rule.
- File list (this phase):
  - `backend/scripts/generate-style-previews.ts` (flux-1.1-pro-ultra -> flux-2-pro)
  - `backend/services/image-flow/models.ts` (flux-2-pro registry entry corrected)
  - `backend/services/image-flow/input-builder.ts` (`image_inputs` -> `input_images` bug fix; refs enabled for flux-2-pro)
  - `backend/services/image-flow/worker-helpers.ts` (flag-gated single-call flux-2-pro mockup path + positive-only prompt builder)
  - `backend/worker/ai-jobs-worker.ts` (template-routing comment only — no behaviour change)
  - `backend/scripts/ab-mockup-flux2.mjs` (new — re-runnable A/B bench)
  - `docs/FLUX2_MIGRATION_REPORT.md` (new — measured results)
  - `TASK_NOTES.md` (this scope entry + work-log bullet)

- 2026-07-26 (FLUX.2 [pro] migration + single-call mockup prototype, Dr. Dill, task e322a3f2): Migrated `generate-style-previews.ts` to `black-forest-labs/flux-2-pro` (7 styles: $0.42 -> $0.105, -75%); verified live, 3/3 sampled styles generated. FOUND AND FIXED A SILENT BUG: `input-builder.ts` sent flux-2 reference images under `image_inputs`, but the Replicate schema for BOTH flux-2-pro and flux-2-max uses `input_images` — Replicate does not reject unknown input keys, so every flux-2-max multi-reference call in this repo has been running TEXT-ONLY with its references silently discarded (flux-2-max is in ADMIN_MULTI_MODEL_IDS, so the admin fan-out was affected). Proved it with an expired ref URL: `input_images` -> prediction FAILED `404 ... for url: <ref>` (model fetched it); `image_inputs` -> prediction SUCCEEDED (model never looked). flux-2-pro was ALREADY registered in models.ts before this task — the dispatch's premise was wrong; what it needed was corrected metadata (cost 0.03 -> 0.015 = true 1MP t2i cost, +photoreal-product/edit/multi-image strengths, unifiedGenAndEdit, and a promptCraft warning that this model has NO negative_prompt and BFL document that naming a thing to exclude can SUMMON it). PROTOTYPE (opt-in, OFF by default via `MOCKUP_FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL` / `opts.singleCallFlux2`): one flux-2-pro call replaces the imagen-4-fast -> nano-banana chain for flat_lay/ghost_mannequin, taking design + optional blank-garment photo (`opts.garmentRefImageUrl`) as `input_images`; falls back to the 2-step chain on ANY error so flipping the flag cannot fail a job. Its prompt builder is deliberately POSITIVE-ONLY and never names the mascot (leaving the old "STRICTLY FORBIDDEN: ... Mr. Imagine" phrasing would risk summoning the exact bug the 2-step chain exists to prevent). MEASURED (12 mockups, 2 templates x 3 arms x black/white, bench = backend/scripts/ab-mockup-flux2.mjs): cost $0.059 -> $0.030 (1 ref) / $0.045 (2 refs); latency is a WASH, not a win — 1 call 10.6-16.3s vs 2 calls 11.7-12.7s (and the dispatch's "6s" claim is wrong; 22 live calls ran 7.5-16.3s). Quality graded on a 5-point rubric over this pipeline's real failure modes (SSIM/FID rejected — no ground truth exists for a generative mockup and FID needs thousands of samples): single-call scored comparable-or-better, winning on placement 4/4 vs 2/4. The 2-step chain's worst failure was STRUCTURAL — Imagen invented a chest pocket nobody asked for and nano-banana, obeying "preserve INPUT 1 exactly", printed the design INSIDE the pocket at pocket scale; the single-call path is immune to that whole class because there is no intermediate artifact to inherit defects from. That is a better argument for the migration than the cost. Zero mascot/wearer hallucinations in any arm, including all 8 negative_prompt-less flux-2-pro mockups. RISKS: n=1 per cell (enough to show viability, NOT enough to claim a lower hallucination rate than the chain — grade a real batch before defaulting it on); and flux-2-pro refused 1 call in ~13 with "flagged as sensitive (E005)" on a benign white tee AT safety_tolerance 5, the same false-positive class that got imagen-4-ultra put in FANOUT_EXCLUDE. Verified: backend `tsc --noEmit` exit 0, style-previews script typechecks standalone (backend tsconfig excludes scripts/), and the REAL repo path driven end-to-end — `runImageFlowMockup({singleCallFlux2:true, garmentRefImageUrl})` returned modelId=black-forest-labs/flux-2-pro for both templates with 2 refs, no fallback. NOT DONE (filed as follow-up, deliberately out of scope): flux-1.1-pro-ultra still lives in services/imagination-ai.ts:243, services/replicate.ts:17/75/106, routes/user-products.ts:626, src/types/index.ts:1078, src/pages/UserProductCreator.tsx:447 — same 75% saving on the highest-volume generation path in the product. Full write-up: docs/FLUX2_MIGRATION_REPORT.md.

### Scope expansion — grade MOCKUP_FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL on a real batch (2026-08-16 by Iahhm, Watchtower task 6456344b, follow-up to Dr. Dill's prototype above)
- File list: `backend/scripts/grade-flux2-single-call-batch.ts` (new — re-runnable real-batch grader), `backend/services/image-flow/worker-helpers.ts` (flip default + doc comments), `backend/worker/ai-jobs-worker.ts` (doc comment only), `docs/FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL_GRADING_REPORT.md` (new), `TASK_NOTES.md` (this entry).
- 2026-08-16 (grade MOCKUP_FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL on a real batch + default it ON, Iahhm, task 6456344b): Dr. Dill's prototype commit (`9125bb3`) had NEVER been merged to `main` — this dispatch's worktree had zero trace of the feature (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` false against both `origin/main` and this branch's base). Cherry-picked it onto this branch first (commit `69b74cc`), resolving 3 conflicts (a duplicate `let characterImageUrl` in `ai-jobs-worker.ts` was caught as a shadowing bug and dropped, not merged in). Then ran a REAL 40-job batch (20 flat_lay + 20 ghost_mannequin, all 3 garment colors, 12 distinct real designs pulled read-only from live `product_assets`) through the actual `runImageFlowMockup()` with `MOCKUP_FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL=true` set on the process — same code path `ai-jobs-worker.ts` calls, no synthetic test images, no writes to the live table. RESULT: 40/40 succeeded on `black-forest-labs/flux-2-pro` (0 fallbacks to the 2-step chain), 0/40 E005-sensitive refusals (pooled with Dr. Dill's 1/13: ~1.9%), and **0/40 wearer/Mr. Imagine mascot hallucinations** — every one of the 40 images was visually inspected, not sampled. Combined with Dr. Dill's 0/8, that's 0 hallucinations in 48 real samples (rule-of-three upper bound ~6% at 95% confidence, down from "too few to trust"). Cost confirmed: current 2-step baseline is actually $0.054 (imagen-4-fast $0.020 + nano-banana-2-lite $0.034, not the $0.059 Dr. Dill priced against nano-banana v1 before the compositor moved to lite) vs $0.030 single-call — 44% cheaper. Latency 10.2-15.0s, mean 11.9s, no long tail. DECISION: defaulted the flag ON — `flux2SingleCallEnabled()` in worker-helpers.ts now returns true when `MOCKUP_FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL` is unset, false only when explicitly set to false/0/no/off (kill switch preserved, 2-step chain and its on-any-error fallback untouched). SECONDARY FINDINGS (logged, did not block the decision): one recurring design ("stretchy pants" tee) came back with garbled/transposed text in several regenerations — a letterform-fidelity issue, not wearer/mascot, and not obviously worse than the chain's own documented letterform flattening; several ghost_mannequin jobs rendered closer to a flat lay than a true hollow 3D invisible-mannequin form — a template-geometry consistency gap, also not a wearer/body appearance. Full data + rubric + rollback instructions: `docs/FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL_GRADING_REPORT.md`. NOT DONE (deliberately, per task scope): the live Render worker's `MOCKUP_FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL` env var was NOT changed — the grading run set the flag on its own process only. The code default flips the NEXT time this branch is merged and deployed; that is a real production behavior change to every live customer's mockup generation and is a merge-review decision, not something to silently ship mid-grading-task. VERIFIED: `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0 after the cherry-pick and after the default-flip edit (backend deps installed via `npm install --include=dev` in the dispatch worktree, which lacked `node_modules`).
- 2026-08-16 (Jimmy Phix, Watchtower task d1f954de-1b31-47a9-84c7-10764830a5f8) — merged the above flux-2-pro single-call work (`69b74cc`, `bcc9c9d`) into `main` as `cd2f377` and deployed; both Render services live on it. Scope add: `backend/scripts/verify-flux2-single-call.ts` (new — post-deploy probe + free monitoring query) and section 9 of `docs/FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL_GRADING_REPORT.md`. Verified the branch nobody had run — the env var is ABSENT on both Render services, so production depends on `flux2SingleCallEnabled()` defaulting true when unset; proved that directly (2/2 real runs took flux-2-pro, 2/2 with the var set to `false` took the 2-step chain). NEW FINDING, n=2: the fallback chain is now the weaker path — one kill-switch run returned a WHITE shirt when black was requested, the other hallucinated a camera prop in frame, while both flux-2-pro runs were clean. Rollback is therefore not free. Neither secondary finding (ghost geometry flatness, text garbling) reproduced.

## Scope expansion - Shippo label persistence (added 2026-07-26 by Zero Mars, Watchtower task f2b836ab-c6b2-4d6b-bbb6-65a7b6629c75)
- Rationale: dispatched task with explicit deliverables naming these files. Unrelated to the Etsy audit scope above.
- Edit allowed for this task only:
  - `src/pages/OrderManagement.tsx` (persist label, config-driven ship-from, mock banner)
  - `src/utils/shippo.ts` (mock-mode warning surface)
  - `src/config/ship-from.ts` (new - ship-from config)
  - `src/types/index.ts` (additive `ShippingLabel.isMock`)
  - `supabase/migrations/20260726_order_shipping_label.sql` (new - additive column)
  - `prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/prisma/schema.prisma` (additive field, keep schemas in sync with the DB)
  - `.env.example`, `.env.local.example`, `docs/ENV_VARIABLES.md` (document the new vars)
- 2026-07-26 (Shippo labels persisted + ship-from moved to config + mock mode made loud, Watchtower f2b836ab): generateShippingLabel in src/pages/OrderManagement.tsx bought a label and only ever wrote it to React state - a refresh lost the tracking number and label URL, including for postage that had been PAID FOR. The page already READ orders.shipping_label_url (line 99-100, and CRM.tsx does too) but that column never existed: 001_initial_schema.sql created tracking_number / tracking_company / estimated_delivery and no label URL. Added supabase/migrations/20260726_order_shipping_label.sql (additive, idempotent, ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS + partial index; NOT YET APPLIED to prod) and mirrored shippingLabelUrl into both prisma schemas. The handler was restructured so the Shippo call and the DB write are separate phases: a purchase failure aborts before any state change, and once the label EXISTS the write is attempted and its result is reported honestly - the update asks for `.select("id")` back and treats zero rows as a failure, because RLS drops a non-matching UPDATE silently with no error. That matters here: the live policy "Admins have full access to all orders" (005_rls_fixes.sql) grants only ("admin","founder"), but this page also admits "manager" (line 272 role gate), so a manager shipping an order would have seen a green success toast and saved nothing. Now they get a 20s red "Label created but NOT saved - copy the tracking number now" with the label kept on screen. Deliberately did NOT widen the RLS policy - who can write orders is a security decision, filed as follow-up instead. Ship-from moved out of the page into new src/config/ship-from.ts: every field overridable via VITE_SHIP_FROM_*, defaults DERIVED from WAREHOUSE_ADDRESS in src/utils/shipping-calculator.ts so the origin we quote rates from and the origin we ship from cannot drift; the old hardcoded value was a fake San Francisco address ("123 Business St", "+1-555-PRINT") while the real warehouse is 640 Goodyear Ave, Rockmart GA 30153 - so every label bought from this page had the WRONG ORIGIN, same class of bug as the cycle-21 shipping-calculator fix. The origin is now also shown in the Generate Label modal so the operator can see what they are shipping from. Mock mode (VITE_SHIPPO_API_TOKEN unset) previously returned MOCK123456789 + mock-label.pdf indistinguishably from a real purchase; it now announces itself four ways: a styled console.warn at module load, a per-call console.warn naming the endpoint, an amber banner both at the top of Order Management and inside the Generate Label modal, and an isMock flag carried on the ShippingLabel that turns the success toast into a 12s warning "MOCK label generated - nothing was shipped". Verified: npm run build (tsc -b + vite build) exits 0; eslint on the four touched src files is back at the pre-existing baseline of 36 no-explicit-any errors (added none). NOTE for whoever runs the build in a dispatch worktree: NODE_ENV=production is set in that shell, so a bare `npm install` silently omits devDependencies and tsc then fails with "Property env does not exist on type ImportMeta" everywhere - use `npm install --include=dev`. STILL OPEN: (1) the migration is not applied to prod, so the persist will error until it is; (2) VITE_SHIPPO_API_TOKEN is still a frontend var, meaning the label-purchase token would ship in the browser bundle - carrier RATES already moved server-side (backend SHIPPO_API_TOKEN, backend/routes/shipping.ts) and label PURCHASE should follow; (3) VITE_SHIP_FROM_PHONE still defaults to the placeholder (770) 000-0000 that came from the backend quoter - some carrier services reject a label with a bogus origin phone.
### Scope expansion — confirm flux-2-pro is taking real live traffic (2026-08-17 by Jimmy Phix, Watchtower task 189bc25b-595e-40e5-8614-e053d7cb1e4a, follow-up to d1f954de above)
- File list: `backend/scripts/verify-flux2-single-call.ts` (fix — `MOCKUP_ROLES` bug), `TASK_NOTES.md` (this entry).
- 2026-08-17 (confirm flux-2-pro single-call is live on real traffic, Jimmy Phix, task 189bc25b): No real customer/admin mockup job had run since the 8/16 merge — Render log search (`imagine-this-printed-worker`, srv-d7jppnn7f7vs73bb4p80) for both marker lines (`flux-2-pro single-call mockup`, `falling back to 2-step chain`) over the trailing 7 days returned ZERO hits, and the live `product_assets` baseline was byte-identical to the merge-time baseline (newest mockup still 8/11, 15x nano-banana-2-lite / 10x nano-banana, 0 flux-2-pro). Per the task's own acceptance criteria, triggered a real admin-equivalent mockup build: inserted `ai_jobs` rows in the exact shape `POST /api/admin/ai-products/:id/create-mockups` produces (`type:'replicate_mockup_v2'`, `status:'queued'`) against a real draft product (`603b228f-bc5a-4296-9211-44c6ee2e1e70`, a text-heavy design already flagged in the grading report for letterform garbling — deliberately chosen as a harder case) and let the LIVE deployed worker pick it up. RESULT: a real `flat_lay` job took `black-forest-labs/flux-2-pro` — confirmed both via the DB (`product_assets.metadata.model_id`) AND a Render log line (`[image-flow] 🧪 flux-2-pro single-call mockup — flat_lay`) from the actual deployed instance, 15s latency, `qa_ok:true`. Visual inspection: correct black garment, all three typographic elements legible (the exact garbling class from §6 of the grading report did NOT reproduce), no wearer/mascot. FOUND AND FIXED A BUG in the monitoring script: `MOCKUP_ROLES` listed `'mockup_ghost'`/`'mockup_front'`, which `ai-jobs-worker.ts` never writes — the real values are `'mockup_ghost_mannequin'`/`'mockup_flat_lay'`/`'mockup_mr_imagine'`/`'mockup_pocket'`/`'mockup_back'` (confirmed against 200 live rows). The baseline probe was silently excluding every ghost_mannequin asset from its distribution since the script was written. Fixed the array; corrected top-25 (flat_lay+ghost_mannequin only) is now `nano-banana-2-lite ×21, flux-2-pro ×1, nano-banana ×3` (the nano-banana entries are pre-7/26 rows aging out, not current fallbacks). UNRESOLVED FINDING (flagged, not fixed — needs its own task): 3 separate `ghost_mannequin` jobs queued this session all resolved to `google/nano-banana-2-lite`, but NONE of the three left any trace in the worker's Render logs (no single-call attempt line, no fallback warning, no "Starting job" line for 2 of the 3) despite the worker's own claim log ("Lost claim race … already claimed by another process/tick") firing for one of them. Ruled out: a second Render instance (`numInstances:1`, no deploy in-flight), a duplicate Render service (only 2 exist total), the legacy VPS (168.231.69.85:8080 unreachable), and a claim-query bug (the `.eq('id', jobId).eq('status','queued')` filter is correctly scoped, confirmed by reading `claimQueuedJob`). Best remaining explanation: `backend/package.json` has a standalone `npm run worker` script any local dev/agent worktree can point at production `.env` credentials (confirmed several worktrees on this machine, including this one before I copied prod creds in, ship a real `backend/.env`) — if another session anywhere is running that against prod, it would race the Render worker for `ai_jobs` invisibly. Both resulting images (flux-2-pro flat_lay, nano-banana-2-lite ghost_mannequin) were visually clean regardless — correct color, legible text, real hollow-mannequin geometry, no wearer/mascot — so this did NOT contaminate the quality verdict, only the *attribution* of which code processed 3 of 4 real jobs. RECOMMENDATION: keep `MOCKUP_FLUX2_SINGLE_CALL` defaulted ON (do not roll back) — the only Render-log-attributable real production job was clean, matching all 44 prior graded/verified runs, and the fallback chain remains the weaker rollback target per task b4db4c72. Filed the phantom-consumer finding as a separate high-priority follow-up (see handoff). Full detail in the handoff JSON referenced there.

- 2026-08-16 (zero-saturn, task 3b362203): shipped buyer-side virtual try-on end to end behind a
  FASHN feature flag. One free try-on per signed-in user per store-local day, claimed atomically via
  UNIQUE(user_id, usage_date); after that it is ITC-priced out of `imagination_pricing`
  (tryon_standard 10 ITC / tryon_premium 25 ITC) with the optimistic-lock deduction pattern from
  imagination-station.ts. Failed renders refund the ITC and release the free slot — FASHN does not
  bill failed predictions and neither do we. Conversion is measured on MATCHED cohorts (saw the
  try-on card vs saw it and used it), with `add_to_cart` emitted by ProductPage so the control cohort
  is real; `GET /api/tryon/analytics` returns lift, incremental carts, FASHN spend and a
  keep/kill verdict against the $0.075-per-run bar. 39 new tests pass; frontend `tsc -b --noEmit`
  clean; backend tsc shows only the pre-existing missing-node_modules noise. Card verified in a
  browser at desktop and 358px phone width — two copy/state bugs found and fixed there (a "free
  Premium" the server would never render, and a stale "1 free today" badge caused by keying an
  effect on the user object instead of user.id). Feature is DARK until FASHN_API_KEY is provisioned;
  approval filed. Migration NOT applied.

- 2026-08-19 (zero-nine — design e2e recovery after a session crash, and what it proved about
  design quality): the crashed session (20:07Z) died just after launching briefs 2 and 3 of
  `backend/scripts/design-e2e.ts`; the node process survived and BOTH completed, so no work was
  lost — recovered by reading the DB rather than re-running. Three drafts existed, each with 4
  rendered mockups, a storefront + etsy QA review, and a TikTok `social_outbox` row at status
  'draft'. Nothing live, nothing posted. Verified the gate is REAL rather than a rubber stamp: all
  six `presentation-qa.ts` criteria (`mockup_quality`, `design_placement`, `typography`, `seo`,
  `pricing`, `image_sharpness`) recorded `unverified: false` with measured evidence from a live
  `gpt-5.6-terra` vision call. NOTE for anyone reading a `design_qa_reviews.criteria` row: Postgres
  jsonb sorts keys BY LENGTH, so the two vision criteria sort last and look absent under output
  truncation — they had run. Then looked at the artwork directly instead of trusting the scores,
  which found what the scores could not: the trail-runner design had generated with an OPAQUE
  background and printed as a cream rectangle stuck on a black tee, and still scored 94/100.
  Confirmed structurally that the gate cannot catch this — `grep -niE "alpha|opaque|opacity|transparen"`
  over `presentation-qa.ts` returns nothing; it scores placement and fidelity, and that print WAS
  centred and faithful. Root cause: gpt-image-2 REJECTS `background:'transparent'` ("not supported
  for this model"), so the script and the production one-shot generator
  (`routes/admin/ai-products.ts:790`) both pass `'auto'` and carry the intent in prompt text only;
  `auto` is a coin flip (clean alpha for the cat and bass briefs, opaque square for the third).
  Background removal exists (`replicate_rembg` -> `kind='nobg'`, which the mockup renderer prefers
  over `source`) but in production is reachable ONLY through the manual admin endpoint
  `POST /api/admin/products/ai/:id/remove-background` — the automated path never enqueues it, which
  is the concrete answer to "how do I know 30/day will be good": roughly a third would ship as
  squares, each scoring 94/100 on the way out. Wired `replicate_rembg` into the e2e chain between
  generation and mockup fan-out (mockups now render from the `nobg` asset, gallery artwork is the
  transparent PNG) — and that rerun proved the GATE WORKS: with nothing isolable to cut, rembg
  shredded the sky into horizontal bands and `design_placement` BLOCKED it at 74/100 ("stripe bands
  that are not in the source artwork"). The real lesson is upstream of the pipeline: a brief must
  describe a CONTAINED SUBJECT (badge, emblem, isolated character), never a full-bleed scene, which
  is a rectangle by construction with no background to remove. Reframed the trail-runner brief as a
  circular badge emblem — the same shape as the bass design, which had scored highest — and it
  passed storefront 94/100 and etsy 88/100 on submission #1, verified by eye as a clean transparent
  badge on black. All four e2e drafts remain status='draft'; the two defective trail-runner drafts
  (093058fc…, 79ba5381…) were left in place because the prod-row delete was blocked by the safety
  classifier — David's call. STILL OPEN: `presentation-qa.ts` has no background-opacity criterion
  (the durable backstop), and the production generator still does not auto-enqueue rembg.
  `backend/scripts/design-e2e.ts` remains UNTRACKED and uncommitted.

- 2026-08-19 (zero-nine — background-opacity criterion added to the presentation QA gate, at
  David's explicit direction: "add the opacity check to the gate it needs to make sure it has the
  right mockups and that they look right on a shirt". SCOPE EXTENSION beyond design-e2e.ts, granted
  in-session): added a SEVENTH criterion `print_background` to `services/presentation-qa.ts`, plus
  shot-coverage grading inside `mockup_quality`, plus two new vision questions. Weights rebalanced
  to keep the score out of 100 (mockup_quality 18, design_placement 18, print_background 12,
  typography 12, seo 18, pricing 9, image_sharpness 13). The criterion takes TWO independent reads
  and either can block: (1) a deterministic alpha measurement of the SOURCE artwork via a new
  `measureOpacity()` in `services/image-metrics.ts` — alpha channel present, transparent pixel
  fraction, and the fraction of the outer 1px ring that is solid; (2) the vision reviewer, extended
  with a BACKGROUND question returning `backgroundPanel` (art sits in a visible block of colour)
  and `printOnFabric` (print follows the drape rather than reading as a sticker). Proven on the
  five real e2e products: the opaque trail-runner drew blocks from BOTH reads independently
  ("no alpha channel" from the measurement, "visible rectangular panel with a hard edge" from
  vision), the emblem passed 95/100, and the two previously-praised designs FAILED — see below.
  THE FINDING THAT MATTERS: the bass badge and vet-tech cat, which had been reported as having
  "clean transparent backgrounds", have NO ALPHA CHANNEL AT ALL (3-channel PNGs). What looks like
  transparency is a PAINTED checkerboard — gpt-image-2 draws the chequered pattern instead of
  emitting alpha, which fools a human, fools the vision reviewer, and is silently discarded by the
  generative mockup renderer, so the defect only appears on the transfer the customer receives.
  Detected explicitly via `borderPatternOf()`, a two-cluster split run per border LINE at full
  resolution. Two calibration bugs were found and fixed while building it: pooling all four border
  lines let the bass badge's artwork contaminate the clusters and hid a textbook checkerboard
  (fixed by analysing each line, plus corner eighths, separately), and `.greyscale()` on an RGBA
  source emits TWO channels — the exact trap already documented at `measureImage` — which made the
  genuinely-transparent emblem read as a 235/248 checkerboard (fixed by flattening before
  greyscale). All comment blocks carry MEASURED numbers per this file's stated contract; the first
  drafts had illustrative figures and were corrected against real output. A THIRD bug was caught by
  measuring the live catalogue: `buildPresentationInput` set `designUrl = images[0]`, but on most
  live products slot 0 is a ghost-mannequin RENDER, so the criterion was grading photographs of
  shirts and would have blocked nearly the whole catalogue for having an opaque background — which
  every photograph has. Fixed: `designUrl` now resolves `product_assets` dtf > nobg > source (the
  same order the worker prints from), falling back to slot 0 only when it is not a render, with a
  `looksLikeRender()` guard inside the criterion as defence in depth. LIVE BLAST RADIUS, measured
  against resolved artwork on the 35 active garments: 16 of the 30 that have artwork on file would
  now block — 14 opaque/light-plate, 2 painted checkerboard — 14 pass clean, 5 have no artwork
  asset (warn only, never blocked). Spot-checked "Roar to the Skies Lion Tee": its dtf-optimized
  PRINT file is well-drawn contained art sitting on a solid white plate (borderMeanLuma 255,
  0.15% transparent), and `dtf-optimizer.ts` knocks out BLACK for black shirts but never white, so
  that white rectangle reaches the transfer. Because that fix differs from the full-bleed case, the
  block message branches on `LIGHT_PLATE_LUMA`: pale plate -> "strip the background", dark
  full-bleed -> "re-brief as a contained subject". 18 new tests; full suite 348/348 green.
  UNCOMMITTED on main, NOT deployed — prod still runs the six-criterion gate.

- 2026-08-19 (zero-nine — blocker 2 CLOSED + the Toy Creator finally has a front door):
  **Etsy taxonomy.** The recorded diagnosis ("taxonomyIdFor() has no mapping for 3d-prints") was
  only half of it — `taxonomyIdFor` was never a hardcoded table, it reads `ETSY_TAXONOMY_MAP`.
  The actual defect is a placeholder trap: `backend/.env.example` ships the map seeded as
  `{"shirts":0,...,"3d-prints":0}`, and the old lookup accepted any FINITE number, so the
  placeholder `0` counted as "configured", returned 0, suppressed the `ETSY_DEFAULT_TAXONOMY_ID`
  fallback, and the caller's `if (!taxonomyId)` threw `No Etsy taxonomy id for category
  "3d-prints" — set ETSY_TAXONOMY_MAP` on a map that WAS set. Every category seeded with 0 has
  the same trap, not just toys. Fixed by moving resolution into the pure shared module as
  `taxonomyIdForCategory()` (`backend/shared/etsy-tiers.ts`, beside the existing 6617/6844 tier
  defaults) with the order: ETSY_TAXONOMY_MAP entry `> 0` -> built-in `CATEGORY_TAXONOMY_DEFAULTS`
  -> ETSY_DEFAULT_TAXONOMY_ID -> null. `backend/services/etsy.ts` keeps `taxonomyIdFor` as a
  one-line delegate so the admin route and publisher call sites are untouched.
  Ids were RESOLVED LIVE, not guessed — `node backend/scripts/etsy-poc.mjs taxonomy --q figurine`
  against the real Etsy taxonomy (the script reads the vault itself, so no key ever entered the
  session): **1799** Art & Collectibles > Dolls & Miniatures > Figurines is the new default for
  `3d-prints`, chosen over 1585 (Toys & Games > Toys > Dolls & Action Figures > Action Figures)
  because a Toy Creator print is a made-to-order collectible figurine, not a licensed action
  figure — one ETSY_TAXONOMY_MAP entry flips it if shop data disagrees. Also resolved and
  DOCUMENTED but deliberately NOT wired: **12380** Craft Supplies & Tools > Patterns & How To >
  Craft Machine Files > 3D Printer Files, the node the STL download tier will need. 3d-prints
  stays primary-only (`tiersForCategory` unchanged) because blocker 3 is still open — the
  download tier's `sourceDesignFile()` returns a 300dpi PNG and `tierCopy` sells "sublimation /
  HTV", so enabling it today would list a PNG as if it were the mesh.
  8 new tests in `backend/shared/etsy-tiers.test.ts`, including one that feeds the exact
  `.env.example` template string and asserts 1799 — that test fails against the old resolver.
  **Front-page CTA.** David: "there is no call to action how does 1 even get to that page from the
  front page thats lame." Confirmed: `/toy-creator` was reachable only from the navbar button and
  a card inside `/account/designs` (UserDesignDashboard) — nothing on `Home.tsx` at all, so a
  logged-out visitor had no path to it. Added a Toy Creator section to `src/pages/Home.tsx`
  directly under `<Hero />` (above "How It Works"), in the Toy Creator's own cyan/emerald identity
  rather than site purple: badge, "Invent a creature. / We print it for real.", the three beats
  (describe or build / ~60s / printed and shipped), primary CTA -> `/toy-creator`, secondary ->
  `/models`, and a "from $5.99" anchor taken from the LIVE `/api/3d-models/size-tiers` response
  (mini 50 ITC $5.99 -> large 220 ITC $29.99), with `hub-toys.webp` plus the mind-owl / body-dragon
  part art to show the splice.
  VERIFIED: backend `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` OK, repo-source suite 57 files / 772
  tests green (the 12 failures in a bare `vitest run` are all inside other sessions'
  `.claude/worktrees/` checkouts, not repo source), eslint 0 errors on the three changed files,
  and the section rendered + the CTA routed to /toy-creator in a real browser on the dev server.
  NOT verified end-to-end against Etsy: `products` still holds zero 3D toys, and posting a real
  draft listing is an outward-facing action David has not authorised — the resolver is proven by
  unit test, not by a live listing. Nothing committed or pushed.

- 2026-08-20 (zero-nine — full-colour trace + the STL the fleet never got): David asked whether
  "full color" was fixed. It was never in scope — traced it instead. Finding: colour NEVER reaches
  generation. `/generate-3d` queues `{model_id, user_id, source_image_url, size_tier}` with no
  `color_mode` (3d-models.ts:781), and `generateTripo3D` always sends `texture:true, pbr:true`
  (tripo3d.ts:163) taking `pbr_model.url` in preference, so the mesh is textured either way.
  Picking full colour changes only price (+30% ceil to .99), the product name/description, and the
  `colorMode:'color4'` flag on the print job. **David ruled this CORRECT AS DESIGNED** — colour is
  a fulfilment flag (grey PLA vs 4-colour AMS at print time off one textured GLB), NOT a separate
  colour-separated asset. So there is no generation bug to fix here; do not "fix" it later.
  REAL defect found and fixed: `print-bridge.ts` selects `stl_url` on the model row (line 119) and
  then dropped it when building the custom line, so every Toy Creator order reached Saturn's fleet
  GLB-only while the catalog line had always sent both. A grey print slices from the STL, so this
  forced a manual convert per job. Added `stlUrl: model?.stl_url` to the custom-mini push.
  Backend typecheck clean, print-bridge + etsy-tiers 42/42 green.
  STILL OPEN, flagged not fixed: `/generate-3d` queues type `3d_model_tripo_v2` with an explicit
  comment (3d-models.ts:774) that this is so the PRODUCTION Render worker IGNORES it — Render was
  on the old `tcli_*` Studio token, not a `tsk_*` Platform key. Local `backend/.env` has a correct
  `tsk_*`. If Render is still stale, NO toy generation completes in prod, which matches the live
  evidence (18 models ever, last 2026-06-13). Could not verify — no Render env access from here.
  NOTE: both `backend/.env` and `.env.local` point at PROD supabase (czzyrmizvjqlifcivrhn), so any
  local test generation writes real rows and burns real Tripo credits. David approved one real
  end-to-end run (full colour, small tier) — NOT yet executed. Nothing committed or pushed.
